# Proposed OEIS comments: A242578 to A250789

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A242578

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A242578>  
Result obtained: **8 September 2026**  
Paper: [10290](../papers/10001-10500/10290-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) - 5*a(n-2) + 6*a(n-3) - 4*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(13 - 17*x + 22*x^2 - 20*x^3) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 3*x + 2*x^2 - 4*x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 01 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 36 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A243022

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A243022>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [10901](../papers/10501-11000/10901-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence: +2*n*(n-1)*(2*n-3)*(5*n-12)*a(n) -(n-1)*(85*n^3-459*n^2+776*n-400)*a(n-1) +4*(-40*n^4+396*n^3-1455*n^2+2324*n-1360)*a(n-2) +4*(n-4)*(170*n^3-1258*n^2+3003*n-2185)*a(n-3) +16*(n-4)*(n-5)*(20*n^2-118*n+143)*a(n-4) -272*(n-4)*(n-5)*(n-6)*(5*n-7)*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jul 15 2017

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 3, so the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10627](../papers/10501-11000/10627-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Nov 02 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:


## A243514

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A243514>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10236](../papers/10001-10500/10236-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 8*a(n-1) - 21*a(n-2) + 22*a(n-3) - 8*a(n-4).
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Nov 02 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A243517

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A243517>  
Result obtained: **8 September 2026**  
Paper: [9336](../papers/09001-09500/09336-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 23*a(n-1) - 212*a(n-2) + 1010*a(n-3) - 2669*a(n-4) + 3887*a(n-5) - 2878*a(n-6) + 840*a(n-7).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(3 - 64*x + 531*x^2 - 2175*x^3 + 4579*x^4 - 4607*x^5 + 1680*x^6) / ((1 - x)^2*(1 - 2*x)*(1 - 3*x)*(1 - 4*x)*(1 - 5*x)*(1 - 7*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 02 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 237 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A243585

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A243585>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [10876](../papers/10501-11000/10876-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence: 2*n*(2*n-1)*(3*n-5)*a(n) +(-123*n^3+328*n^2-249*n+60)*a(n-1) +50*(n-1)*(2*n-3)*(3*n-2)*a(n-2)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 14 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A243728>  
Result obtained: **8 September 2026**  
Paper: [7747](../papers/07501-08000/07747-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 9*a(n-1) -203*a(n-3) +336*a(n-4) +1869*a(n-5) -4354*a(n-6) -9225*a(n-7) +25044*a(n-8) +27419*a(n-9) -76104*a(n-10) -53277*a(n-11) +119771*a(n-12) +68688*a(n-13) -77292*a(n-14) -45360*a(n-15)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 385 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 0. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A243760

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A243760>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [10895](../papers/10501-11000/10895-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence: 245*n*(n-1)*(n+1)*a(n) -140*n*(n-1)*(28*n-53)*a(n-1) +4*(n-1)*(5812*n^2-29864*n+36585)*a(n-2) +16*(-3368*n^3+35040*n^2-111784*n+110667)*a(n-3) +128*(-152*n^3-1224*n^2+14756*n-29655)*a(n-4) +2048*(2*n-9)*(74*n^2-567*n+988)*a(n-5) -98304*(n-6)*(2*n-9)*(2*n-11)*a(n-6)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jun 07 2016

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 5, so the recurrence holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A245088

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A245088>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [11896](../papers/11501-12000/11896-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: All the terms are odd integers. Moreover, a(p) == 3 (mod p) for any prime p.
> In 2015 Guo, Mao and Pan confirmed the conjecture partially by proving that n*a(n) is always an integer. - _Zhi-Wei Sun_, Nov 14 2015
> Conjecture: +n^2*(23656*n-2233)*(n-1)*a(n) +(n-1)*(23656*n^3+655645*n^2-2273556*n+1915600)*a(n-1) -2*(n-2) *(177048*n^3-3748011*n^2+13870329*n-15407140)*a(n-2) -2*(n-3) *(2020728*n^3-18138829*n^2+63885382*n-73802216)*a(n-3) -5*(n-4) *(782552*n^3-11993599*n^2+55520403*n-80051816)*a(n-4) +125*(66088*n-203507)*(n-5)*(n-4)^2*a(n-5)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jul 12 2014

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the recurrence already stated on the entry, which is not labelled a conjecture: in the Ore algebra Q(n)[N], N the shift, the conjectured operator is a left multiple of that one, so it annihilates the sequence as well. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A247170

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A247170>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [11557](../papers/11501-12000/11557-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectured: +n*(2*n-7)*a(n) +(n-1)*(2*n-9)*a(n-1) +2*(-2*n^2+9*n-6)*a(n-2) +2*(-6*n^2+33*n-38)*a(n-3) +3*(-2*n^2+15*n-26)*a(n-4) +(2*n-5)*(n-5)*a(n-5) = 0.

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 4, so the recurrence holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 50 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 52 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 50, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 32 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A250654

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250654>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4115](../papers/04001-04500/04115-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) - 2*a(n-2); also a(n) = 2^(n-1)*36 + (5*2^(n-1)-1)*6 + 2^(n+1).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(32 - 29*x) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 2*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 15 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 128 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A250657

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

```

## A250679

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250679>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11426](../papers/11001-11500/11426-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 58 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 96 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 58, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical g.f.: x*(33 - 47*x - 66*x^2 + 131*x^3 - 41*x^4 - 23*x^5 + 14*x^6 - 2*x^7) / ((1 - x)^2*(1 - 2*x - 3*x^2 + 5*x^3 + x^4 - x^5)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 16 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 16 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A250737

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A250737>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4155](../papers/04001-04500/04155-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 3*a(n-1) - 2*a(n-2); a(n) = 2^(n+1) + 14.
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(9 - 16*x) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 2*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 16 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 16 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) - 5*a(n-2) + 2*a(n-3); a(n) = 36*2^(n-1) + 10*n + 22.
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(34 - 79*x + 40*x^2) / ((1 - x)^2*(1 - 2*x)). - _Colin Barker_, Nov 18 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 32 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
